## 2010 United States National Championship

![](https://i.ormos.uk/e6/52/e652ad7b1a635e5f53f3db0759240500309a00173cde454712e069134ef1ac2c.jpg)

And there we go! After three days of play, the United States has a new national champion! Josh Utter-Leyton had a long road to victory, but his Standard play carried him all the way to the finals without dropping a match. Joining him on this year’s US National team are his finals opponent, Anthony Eason, who burned through the field with his monored Standard deck and two red-based Draft decks on the way to his second place finish, and Conrad Kolos, who had to defeat the top-seeded David Ochoa in the third place playoff to earn his spot. All in all, the United States has put together quite the solid team. We’ll have to wait until December to see if they are able to make a splash at Worlds in Chiba!

*   **[Live Coverage at ggslive.com](http://ggslive.com/show_ggslive.html)**

## PTQ Qualifier: Journeying to Somewhere - Decklists

*by Adam Styborski*

Yesterday's PTQ ran quite a bit longer than I was able to stay at the hall, but thanks to the wonderful judge staff on hand I have some juicy information for you: decklists. Here are 7 of the Top 8 and their Draft Decks:

#### Kevin Hunt

## Quarterfinals: (Flash)freeze, You Bushwhacker! - Anthony Eason vs. Tim Sussino

*by Monty Ashley*

Here we go! The quarterfinal round of the 2010 United States National Championship! But before we start the action, let's take a moment to get to know the competitors' decks.

Anthony Eason's deck is one of those "Red Deck Wins" combinations of "burn" and "more burn" that's been popular for years and years. It has 20 slots devoted to direct damage and another 12 slots devoted to creatures with haste, which are a lot like direct damage spells. There are also four [Goblin Guide](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Guide)s and a some utility lands. It's straightforward and vicious, and it took Eason to an undefeated record in the Standard rounds, winning six and drawing twice.

 

#### Anthony Eason

Summons";s:22:"field\_card\_image\_front";a:0:{}}}} 

 

Tim Sussino has a [Pyromancer Ascension](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyromancer+Ascension) deck, which attempts to get double duty out of [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil), [See Beyond](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=See+Beyond), and [Time Warp](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Time+Warp). And a duplicated [Call to Mind](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Call+to+Mind) can provide the all-important recursion. He has no creatures in his main deck, so Eason will be free to point all of his burn directly at Sussino's face.

Pierce";s:22:"field\_card\_image\_front";a:0:{}}}} 

 

Eason got here by starting strong with a 7-0 record on Day 1. Sussino started 4-3 and needed a 7-0 record on Day 2 to make the Top 8. On Friday night, he didn't think he had a chance, so he spent the evening carousing. On Saturday night, he decided not to mess with a plan that worked and went carousing again.

### Game 1

Eason's starts can be divided into those with [Goblin Guide](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Guide) and those without. This one was without, and Sussino had time to play [Ponder](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ponder) on turn one and [Pyromancer Ascension](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyromancer+Ascension) on turn two because the other player's deck started to affect him. But at the end of Sussino's second turn, Eason started the onslaught, hitting Sussino with a [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) (taking him to 17) and then untapping and playing a [Hell's Thunder](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hell%27s+Thunder) that brought Sussino to 13.

 

![Hell's Thunder](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Hell%27s+Thunder)

![Pyromancer Ascension](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Pyromancer+Ascension)

Sussino stayed cool and stuck with his plan, playing a second [Ponder](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ponder) to put a counter on [Pyromancer Ascension](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyromancer+Ascension). Eason had another [Hell's Thunder](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hell%27s+Thunder) to bring Sussino to 9. The players swapped face-bound [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt)s, and Eason was now ahead 15-6. However, Sussino had a full hand while Eason was down to two cards and was in danger of running out of gas.

Eason unearthed a [Hell's Thunder](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hell%27s+Thunder) to bring Sussino down to 2. Sussino threw a [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) at Eason and then used a [Scalding Tarn](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scalding+Tarn) to get an [Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Island), putting himself at a precarious 1 life. But the [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) had put a second counter on his [Pyromancer Ascension](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyromancer+Ascension) and now he could play a [Time Warp](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Time+Warp) that gave him two extra turns. "I think I won most of my matches at one life yesterday. Hopefully I can do the same," commented Sussino as he started his first extra turn.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/51/3d/513d13fb1d615037e2e60fc7fa2c275ffea03f65bbb3a12bd1fd527b3b70cad4.jpg)

First, he played a (doubled) [See Beyond](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=See+Beyond) and a second [Pyromancer Ascension](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyromancer+Ascension). On his second extra turn, he [Time Warp](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Time+Warp)ed again. The first of those extra turns featured a [Burst Lightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Burst+Lightning), which was kicked and doubled, killing Eason.

Tim Sussino had survived Eason's direct damage assault. Could he do it again?

Tim Sussino 1 - Anthony Eason 0

Between games, Sussino chatted with Eason, suggesting that they tell each other what their sideboard plans were. Eason rejected this proposal and a mock-outraged Sussino protested, "I thought we were playing fairsies!"

### Game 2

This was one of the "Starts with [Goblin Guide](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Guide)" games for Eason. His turns looked like this:

Turn One: [Goblin Guide](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Guide), attack (Sussino at 18)

Turn Two: [Goblin Bushwhacker](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Bushwhacker), attack (Sussino at 13)

Turn Three: [Goblin Bushwhacker](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Bushwhacker), attack (Sussino at 6)

At this point, Sussino began to mount a defense. He [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt)ed the [Goblin Guide](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Guide), [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak)ed a [Staggershock](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Staggershock) and [Burst Lightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Burst+Lightning)ed a [Goblin Bushwhacker](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Bushwhacker) so that the next attack only took him down to 5. But Eason had another [Staggershock](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Staggershock), which knocked Sussino to 3, then 1 on the rebound. It almost didn't matter what Eason drew at that point, and some form of lightning proceeded to kill Sussino.

Tim Sussino 1 - Anthony Eason 1

### Game 3

Sussino got a turn-two [Pyromancer Ascension](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyromancer+Ascension), but Eason had a turn-two [Hellspark Elemental](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hellspark+Elemental). After it was unearthed, Sussino was down to 14. But Sussino's sideboarded [Flashfreeze](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flashfreeze) came in handy for the next [Hellspark Elemental](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hellspark+Elemental). And a [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) came in handy for Eason's [Goblin Bushwhacker](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Bushwhacker).

The Elemental that had been [Flashfreeze](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flashfreeze)d came back from the dead to hit Sussino to 11, and a [Hell's Thunder](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hell%27s+Thunder) brought him to 7. Sussino continued to draw, play a land, and pass the turn. A [Flashfreeze](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flashfreeze) on an end-step [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) gave him one counter on the [Pyromancer Ascension](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyromancer+Ascension).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/9b/48/9b48472d7af56aba10f7fe6d639f68409c734a19d5eb5a2a2dab7c051098c1e5.jpg)

Eason pointed a [Staggershock](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Staggershock) at Sussino, and Sussino responded with a [Spell Pierce](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Pierce). But it was time for Sussino's sideboard to come in handy, as a [Ricochet Trap](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ricochet+Trap) redirected the counter to itself, leaving the [Staggershock](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Staggershock) free to deal a total of 4 damage. With Sussino at 3, Eason tried to put the game away with [Hell's Thunder](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hell%27s+Thunder). But Sussino had a kicked [Burst Lightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Burst+Lightning) to kill the creature and put a second counter on [Pyromancer Ascension](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyromancer+Ascension). With his engine fueled up, would Sussino be able to pull out another win with a dangerously low life total?

No. Eason had a [Manabarbs](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Manabarbs) to get in the way of Sussino coming back, and a kicked [Goblin Bushwhacker](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Bushwhacker) put him in the land of no return.

Anthony Eason 2 - Tim Sussino 1

Although his back was against the wall, Sussino was still optimistic. He'd started US Nationals 0-2 and lost the last round of Day 1, but had won seven in a row when he had two. But on looking at his hand, the normally chatty Sussino seemed to shut down. He looked through his hand and decided that he had to mulligan. "It's not looking good, boys," he told the accumulated spectators.

### Game 4

Sussino started with a [Mountain](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mountain), which let him [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt) Eason's first-turn [Goblin Guide](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Guide). On turn two, he allowed a [Hellspark Elemental](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hellspark+Elemental) to come out and knock him to 17.

Sussino played [Preordain](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Preordain) and commented, "I'm not gonna lie to you. If I win this one, I'm gonna pull it out of my expletive deleted." He actually said "expletive deleted" there, which this reporter appreciates.

Eason's third turn saw a [Goblin Guide](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Guide) and the unearther [Hellspark Elemental](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hellspark+Elemental). Sussino sent the elemental [Into the Roil](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Into+the+Roil) (and out of the game, thanks to the rules of unearth) but took the two damage from the goblin. He was at 15.

Sussino [Ponder](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ponder)ed, both in the sense that he cast the spell by that name and in the sense that he thought about his options. When Eason played [Hell's Thunder](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hell%27s+Thunder), Sussino responded with [Flashfreeze](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flashfreeze).

The [Goblin Guide](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Guide) attacked again. Sussino turned a [Scalding Tarn](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Scalding+Tarn) into a [Mountain](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mountain), going down to 14 in the process. He threw a [Burst Lightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Burst+Lightning) on the [Goblin Guide](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Guide), but Eason responded with a devastating [Ricochet Trap](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ricochet+Trap), redirecting it to Sussino himself. The two damage from the [Burst Lightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Burst+Lightning) and the two from the [Goblin Guide](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Guide) meant that Sussino was down to 10.

Sussino's situation was desperate. Eason unearthed his [Hell's Thunder](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hell%27s+Thunder) and sent it with the [Goblin Guide](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Guide) into the red zone. Sussino was at 4. He used a [Call to Mind](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Call+to+Mind) to bring back his [Burst Lightning](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Burst+Lightning) to kill the [Goblin Guide](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goblin+Guide).

Both players were low on cards and took a few turns to reload. Sussino got a [Pyromancer Ascension](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pyromancer+Ascension). Eason tried for a [Hell's Thunder](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hell%27s+Thunder), but Sussino had a [Flashfreeze](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flashfreeze). Unfortunately for Sussino, he didn't have anything to deal with the unearth ability, and the reborn [Hell's Thunder](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hell%27s+Thunder) killed him.

Anthony Eason 3 - Tim Sussino 1

## Quarterfinals: A Mythic Tale – Gerard Fabiano vs. Josh Utter-Leyton

*by Dave Guskin*

Josh Utter-Leyton, also known as Wrapter and sometime-columnist with the ChannelFireball crew, had another excellent performance here at U.S. Nationals, hot on the heels of his PT San Juan quarterfinalist finish. His opponent, Gerard Fabiano, master storyteller and smartly dressed for his Top 8 appearance, is back in the spotlight after a number of reasonable but not amazing finishes on the GP circuit. Utter-Leyton brought a CFB weapon of choice, the Mythic Conscription deck, against Fabiano's more traditional UW control deck. The matchup revolved, as so many blue-based matches do, around threats, feints, answers and trumps, including a host of counter**Magic** on both sides of the table.

Game 1

![](https://i.ormos.uk/c2/b7/c2b7e3fa18da1273673e86de267032f6bd756694d0fe2e8506ea17ee851cb9d1.jpg)

Utter-Leyton began with a [Misty Rainforest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Misty+Rainforest) into [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest) and [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch) – just the kind of start he was looking for against Fabiano's control deck. Fabiano had only a [Celestial Colonnade](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Colonnade), which meant the next turn's attack met no resistance – he fell to 19.

Josh laid down a [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise), accelerating quickly into his deck's namesake mythic rares – he started the parade with an Elspeth, which met an immediately demise to Fabian's [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak).

Now at five mana sources to Fabiano's three, Utter-Leyton laid a sixth land and slammed a [Sovereigns of Lost Alara](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sovereigns+of+Lost+Alara) onto the battlefield. Fabiano thought for a bit then motioned to let it resolve. He had no [Day of Judgment](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Day+of+Judgment) on his turn, merely passing it back to the now active Sovereigns.

[Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise) crashed into the red zone, and the Sovereigns conscripted it into a monstrous 12/13 flying trample Bird Eldrazi. Fabiano had the answer however, in the form of a [Condemn](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Condemn), and the life totals now stood at Utter-Leyton 32, Fabiano 19.

Fabiano untapped and laid down a [Gideon Jura](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gideon+Jura), who mandated that all creatures on Utter-Leyton's side attack him rather than his master. Utter-Leyton was fine with that, tapping his two mana creatures pre-combat so as to maintain exalted, and the Sovereigns crashed in, searching up a second copy of [Eldrazi Conscription](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eldrazi+Conscription). Gideon died a painful death.

When Fabiano attempted another planeswalker, this time [Jace, the Mind Sculptor](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%2C+the+Mind+Sculptor), Utter-Leyton had a [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak). This left Fabiano with only a [Spreading Seas](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spreading+Seas) to knock one of Utter-Leyton's two [Stirring Wildwood](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stirring+Wildwood)s down to [Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Island) status.

Utter-Leyton untapped and attacked with the Eldrazi-Sovereigns and the other [Stirring Wildwood](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stirring+Wildwood). Fabiano sacrificed his [Spreading Seas](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spreading+Seas) and a [Plains](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plains) to Annihilator, then took 17 in the face down to just 2 life. The UW control player tried a desperation [Baneslayer Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Baneslayer+Angel), but Utter-Leyton continued to smash with his monsters and Fabiano quickly fell to trample damage.

Utter-Leyton 1 – 0 Fabiano

"Hey Josh," Gerard said, as the two quarterfinalists shuffled up for the next game. "You have to mulligan so that I can tell the Conley Woods story."

"The Conley Woods story? What is it?"Josh replied.

"Ah-ah! You have to mulligan," said Fabiano. "It's a small price to pay."

Game 2

![](https://i.ormos.uk/7a/fd/7afda29c66456532c5284e2382d4c005feb57ab696fc2b353938dfd8330dedf8.jpg)

Fabiano led with [Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Island) to Utter-Leyton's tapped Colonnade, which quickly itself became an [Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Island) when the Seas began to Spread. The process repeated the next turn, as Utter-Leyton dropped another Colonnade and Fabiano a [Spreading Seas](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spreading+Seas) for it. The two made a couple more land drops – [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest) for Utter-Leyton and [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge) for Fabiano – but then ran out of lands. Both were forced to discard, a [Day of Judgment](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Day+of+Judgment) and an [Eldrazi Conscription](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eldrazi+Conscription) hitting their respective bins.

Fabiano finally found some action and attempted a [Jace, the Mind Sculptor](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%2C+the+Mind+Sculptor), which ran into [Spell Pierce](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Pierce) from Utter-Leyton. This opened a window for Utter-Leyton to resolve his own [Jace, the Mind Sculptor](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%2C+the+Mind+Sculptor), [Brainstorm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Brainstorm), and then prepare a [Marsh Flats](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Marsh+Flats) to shuffle away the chaff. Fabiano had the answer in the form of [Oblivion Ring](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Oblivion+Ring).

Josh now had white mana and new cards to see from his fetchland. He used it to cast a [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary) and then laid down another white source: [Stirring Wildwood](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stirring+Wildwood). That allowed him to cast Elspeth on the next turn, and he added a [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch) to his ever-growing army. Still, Fabiano had the answers, with another O-Ring for the Knight and an Elspeth of his own to bin both.

Utter-Leyton was unconcerned, however, since his board presence now meant that the [Sovereigns of Lost Alara](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sovereigns+of+Lost+Alara) in his hand represented an additional 11 damage. An already Exalted Soldier bashed in for 13 total due to its Eldrazification, and Fabiano dropped precipitously to 7. Unsurprisingly, Fabiano continued to have the correct trump, this time in [Day of Judgment](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Day+of+Judgment).

Josh rebuilt his position with two Knights of the Reliquary, each 6/6 and ready to crunch into their control opponent. Gerard dropped his personal bodyguard, Gideon, who laid down the law and ensured the Knights would attack him. Josh shrugged, laid down a planeswalker of his own – Jace – and smashed Gideon. Gerard had a [Condemn](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Condemn) for one Knight, but couldn't use [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge) to stop the attacking [Stirring Wildwood](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stirring+Wildwood) because Josh's [Spell Pierce](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Pierce) to stop [Condemn](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Condemn) tapped him out. The land and the other Knight made short work of Jura.

Fabiano had a [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) ready for Elspeth on the next turn, and his [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge) finally dealt with one [Stirring Wildwood](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stirring+Wildwood), but a newly minted [Celestial Colonnade](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Colonnade) and the remaining Knight were too much to handle and he fell in the combat phase.

Utter-Leyton 2 – 0 Fabiano

Game 3

"Well, this hand is the blade, but I want to hear the Conley Woods story," Utter-Leyton declared. "I'll mulligan."

![](https://i.ormos.uk/ab/10/ab10c67479fdd81b195b598ebbd706c3b2aec7e22cf93504863e0a032a7861e3.jpg)

"Yesss!" cried Fabiano, jumping up to regale the crowd with his tale. Although the story loses a lot of its charm in print, it is included here for completeness and hilarity.

"So it's Worlds, and Conley Woods needs two wins in the Extended portion. He decides, alright, I'll play Zoo, that's a solid deck that'll get me the two wins."

"He goes 1-4 and he's going into the last match, has to win to make top eight. He gets paired against a Burn player, and the Burn player mulligans to three. Conley is focused, he is ready. His opponent lays down a [Mountain](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mountain). He goes \[throaty voice\] fetchland, crack it, shockland, pay 2 life, [Kird Ape](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kird+Ape). His opponent shrugs and plays another [Mountain](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mountain). \[throaty voice\] Fetchland, crack it, shockland, pay 2 life, 1/2 [Tarmogoyf](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tarmogoyf), attack, go. His opponent sighs and plays a [Great Furnace](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Furnace)."

"Conley untaps, goes \[throaty voice\] fetchland, crack it, shockland, pay 2 life, [Molten Rain](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Molten+Rain) your [Great Furnace](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Great+Furnace). His opponent sits up, responds with [Shrapnel Blast](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Shrapnel+Blast), putting down to 6. Opponent untaps and casts double [Lightning Bolt](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lightning+Bolt)."

The crowd burst into laughter, and the story drew chuckles from Josh just as he finished his shuffle and drew his six. Gerard took his first turn and [Preordain](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Preordain)ed into [Preordain](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Preordain), and the mana on the board was mirrored – an [Island](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Island) and a Colonnade each. Gerard cast his third [Preordain](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Preordain).

Josh had no plays, stuck on three lands, but Gerard had a [Linvala, Keeper of Silence](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Linvala%2C+Keeper+of+Silence) – an excellent foil to the mana creatures in Josh's deck. Josh had the [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak), however. Gerard had more tricks up his sleeve, with an Elspeth and a horde of tokens.

Utter-Leyton could only watch helpless as Fabiano cast [Meddling Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Meddling+Mage) on [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) and [Jace, the Mind Sculptor](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%2C+the+Mind+Sculptor) in quick succession. Fabiano Fatesealed his manascrewed opponent, putting a [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest) on top.

"Oh, I get to see now," said Utter-Leyton, playing and cracking a [Verdant Catacombs](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Verdant+Catacombs). Sure enough, Fabiano's Jace had put [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest) on bottom. "You don't even have to shuffle!" exclaimed Fabiano.

Despite the slight increase in mana, Utter-Leyton's first turn [Celestial Colonnade](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Colonnade) became a liability as Fabiano tec-edged it. With an active Elspeth and Jace, Fabiano had a commanding lead and Utter-Leyton quickly scooped it up.

Utter-Leyton 2 – 1 Fabiano

"Was your hand actually the stone blade?" Fabiano inquired, curious.

"Heh, no," replied Utter-Leyton. "It was five land and two [Eldrazi Conscription](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eldrazi+Conscription). Go big!"

Game 4

Utter-Leyton, now again on the play, had a turn three [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary), to which Fabiano had no response. Fabiano did, however, have a Jace on his turn, which he ticked up out of killing range from the 3/3 Knight.

Josh still went ahead and activated his [Stirring Wildwood](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stirring+Wildwood), sending both at the blue 'walker. Jace died, but a Linvala took his place, transforming the subtle knight into just an efficient beatdown machine.

Resigned to its purpose, the Knight was joined by a Hierarch and the exalted hero flew over to smack Gerard down to 16. Elspeth joined the UW control side, and so did Soldier token, but Josh just continued to grow his army, adding a second and then a third [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary) to his board.

Still, with only one fetchland in his graveyard, his Knights were more like [Trained Armodon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Trained+Armodon)s. The three plus a [Stirring Wildwood](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stirring+Wildwood) all attacked Elspeth, and Linvala, back on defense, chomped on one of the Knights before the Knight-Errant went to the graveyard.

Fabiano had a Baneslayer to join his Angel armada, and Linvala bashed Utter-Leyton down to 16 with Linvala. Fabiano landed another Jace, and saw an [Eldrazi Conscription](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eldrazi+Conscription) on top of Utter-Leyton's library. He left it there, and sent Baneslayer, putting life totals at 21-11 in Fabiano's favor. An Exalted Knight attempted to murder Jace, but got [Condemn](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Condemn)ed with the exalted trigger on the stack, putting the life totals at 21-14. Utter-Leyton cast Jace's doppelganger, which did what the Knight could not – both Jaces were placed into the graveyard.

![Baneslayer Angel](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Baneslayer+Angel)

![Linvala, Keeper of Silence](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Linvala%2C+Keeper+of+Silence)

The Angels rumbled over again, putting Fabiano up to 26 and Utter-Leyton down to just 6. Fabiano's [Meddling Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Meddling+Mage) named [Jace, the Mind Sculptor](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%2C+the+Mind+Sculptor), and Utter-Leyton appeared out of options. When another Elspeth arrived to send a Soldier to the skies alongside its Angelic brethren, Utter-Leyton packed it in.

Utter-Leyton 2 – 2 Fabiano

Game 5

Utter-Leyton had yet another mulligan to kick off the final game. Fabiano quickly stood, pacing and thinking about his opener. "Do we get another story?" asked a member of the crowd. Everyone laughed.

Josh led with a [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise) into [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary). Gerard, uncharacteristically, had a turn two play, with [Meddling Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Meddling+Mage) naming [Jace, the Mind Sculptor](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Jace%2C+the+Mind+Sculptor). With no lands in his graveyard, Josh sat back with his Knight and passed the turn back to Gerard.

Fabiano opted to cast another [Meddling Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Meddling+Mage), but Utter-Leyton had the [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak) for that one. On end step, [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary) did some exploring, cashing in a [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest) for a [Verdant Catacombs](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Verdant+Catacombs) for another [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest), growing to 5/5.

Fabiano had a third copy of the [Meddle](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Meddle)r, which resolved. He named Sovereigns, and then found a fourth land – a [Celestial Colonnade](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Celestial+Colonnade) – and passed. Utter-Leyton activated the Knight again and found a [Stirring Wildwood](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stirring+Wildwood). He bashed past the two Mages, sacrificing his [Marsh Flats](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Marsh+Flats) for another white source, and put the life totals at 17-14 in his favor.

Gerard cast an [Oblivion Ring](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Oblivion+Ring) to take care of the Knight, but Josh had [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak). Gerard had [Negate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Negate) for the [Mana Leak](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mana+Leak), but off of his last blue source – the initial Birds – Josh cast [Spell Pierce](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Spell+Pierce). The Knight remained on active duty. Gerard bashed in with both Mages, sending Josh to 13, but the subsequent attack of Knight plus activated Colonnade put Josh up 13-6.

![Jace, the Mind Sculptor](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Jace%2C+the+Mind+Sculptor)

![Meddling Mage](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Meddling+Mage)

Utter-Leyton sent in again with the Knight, and [Meddling Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Meddling+Mage) on Jace blocked – Fabiano was hoping that the Jace he'd drawn could dig him out of this mess. However, the Mage's death allowed Utter-Leyton to cast his own Jace post-combat. When Fabiano tried to survive with a [Baneslayer Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Baneslayer+Angel), Utter-Leyton merely used Jace's -1 to bounce it and Fabiano scooped them up.

Utter-Leyton 3 – 2 Fabiano

## Quarterfinal: Land Grab – John Kolos vs. Eric Froehlich

*by Adam Styborski*

**Game 1**

Froehlich placed 37 at Pro Tour San Diego and Kolos hit 20 at last year's Worlds. Both, however, were looking to lock in far higher, and for far more glory, here at Nationals.

"You're like a top 100 match celebrity." Kolos opened.

"Yeah, something like that." Froehlich beamed back.

A best of five match between Froehlich's [Destructive Force](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Destructive+Force) and Kolos's Eldrazi Ramp was underway, Kolos leading with a pair of [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest)s into [Rampant Growth](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rampant+Growth) for yet another [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest). Froehlich's starting [Stirring Wildwood](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stirring+Wildwood) was matched with a [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest) as well.

Kolos continued the ramping with a [Cultivate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cultivate) for a pair of the green making basic land, both ending up in play. Froehlich took a point from his [Arid Mesa](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arid+Mesa), finding a [Mountain](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mountain), and played [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary) – the first nonland permanent of the game. Kolos played only an [Everflowing Chalice](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Everflowing+Chalice) with two counters before passing back.

After a cards in hand check, Froehlich tapped his [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest) for mana and burned it with the Knight for [Verdant Catacombs](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Verdant+Catacombs) which then fetched a [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest). He then followed with [Ajani Vengeant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Vengeant), which took one of Kolos's five [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest)s offline, and a [Raging Ravine](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raging+Ravine).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/98/1b/981bf697e67da1cfb2da47dbaf68278d5df1213ea18ec0508bc791d412cdccb9.jpg)*Each side dueled for tempo in their own ways.*

Kolos spent a moment thinking, and chose to deploy a [Primeval Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Primeval+Titan) – a beast currently burlier than the Knight – that dumped two [Eldrazi Temple](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eldrazi+Temple)s onto the battlefield. Froehlich furrowed his brow as his turn came back. He added a [Plains](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plains), then [Gideon Jura](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gideon+Jura), to his side of things. He then locked down the [Everflowing Chalice](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Everflowing+Chalice) as well as forced the Titan to come barreling into Gideon.

Kolos sent the Titan in, now fetching out two [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge)s. Froehlich chose to block with the Knight and cash out his [Plains](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plains) for an [Arid Mesa](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arid+Mesa) which found a [Plains](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plains) again, and put the Knight up to 7/7. The Titan now gone, Kolos played another Chalice for another two counters and his follow up All is Dust cleared Froehlich's side of things not land.

Froehlich could only play a [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest) and [Rampant Growth](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rampant+Growth) for a [Plains](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plains) before activating the Wildwood and sending it in. Kolos was now untapped with two active Edges and tons of mana at his disposal. The Edges tore away Froehlich's Wildwood and Ravine before Kolos played [Ulamog, the Infinite Gyre](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ulamog%2C+the+Infinite+Gyre) which tagged one of Froehlich's [Plains](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plains).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/0a/bf/0abf8fb656dd3d978a702d0962300353a3160d4d1d153b13ec3573b096c0c6b8.jpg)*Froehlich was dutifully thoughtful about every play.*

Froehlich could only play a land and pass. Kolos sent the Eldrazi into Froehlich's [Path to Exile](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Path+to+Exile) – no surprise with Kolos's choice for [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest) – then played his second [Primeval Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Primeval+Titan). This time his picks were an [Eye of Ugin](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eye+of+Ugin) and a third [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge).

Kolos passed as Froehlich checked the text on the Eye. With that, Froehlich decided it was time to move onto to game 2.

Froehlich 0 – Kolos 1

**Game 2**

It was eerily quiet as both made their sideboarding choices. Both players moved with careful intent and weren't saying anything.

Both kept their openers and Froehlich led with [Raging Ravine](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raging+Ravine). Kolos matched it with a [Khalni Garden](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Khalni+Garden). An [Arid Mesa](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arid+Mesa) joined the Ravine, and [Eldrazi Temple](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eldrazi+Temple) fell into behind the Plant token. Froehlich used the Mesa to find a [Plains](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plains), dropped a [Mountain](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mountain) and brought out the girl herself: [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary).

Kolos's new [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest) looked a bit lonely as his play before he passed back. Froehlich added a [Stirring Wildwood](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stirring+Wildwood) before sending the Knight into Kolos's Plant. Another [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest) was Kolos only play again. Froehlich used the Knight to do the [Plains](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plains)-to-[Arid Mesa](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arid+Mesa)\- to-[Plains](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plains) dance before deploying tectonic Edge and a Realm Razor.

Kolos could only do as before, play a [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest), and passed back to the dominant Froehlich who wasted no time dropping Kolos to 10. Checking one last draw Kolos decided to move on from the quick game.

Froehlich 1 – Kolos 1

Game 3

Froehlich didn't need any of the planeswalker friends to get back into the match. Yet despite the even score, both players weren't easing their intensity any.

Kolos was on the play again but had to move down to 6 for an opening hand. [Khalni Garden](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Khalni+Garden) led the way before Froehlich played his land too. Kolos played a tectonic Edge an [Joraga Treespeaker](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Joraga+Treespeaker). Froehlich just played a [Plains](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plains) and passed back to Kolos who added a counter to the Treespeaker and cast [Rampant Growth](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rampant+Growth) for a [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest).

Froehlich added a second [Plains](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Plains) and dropped an [Oblivion Ring](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Oblivion+Ring) to take the Treespeaker to exile.

"It doesn't come back with the level counters, does it?" Kolos asked.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/2e/07/2e077bd29e99554659e5083f7c98d8b196485c98f79ed84e532195ac7a523a63.jpg)*Kolos, too, kept his wits about him every moment of the match.*

"No, but he would tell you anyway." Froehlich clarified, indicating the judge.

Kolos had a [Cultivate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cultivate) for a deuce of [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest)s, both coming into play. Froehlich meanwhile had a tectonic Edge and [Trace of Abundance](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Trace+of+Abundance) into [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary). Kolos had a nature's [Abundance](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Abundance) for the Trace at the end of Froehlich's turn, then added an [Everflowing Chalice](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Everflowing+Chalice) for two during his own.

Froehlich then sent the Knight in and played an [Arid Mesa](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arid+Mesa) to find a [Mountain](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mountain) – Realm Razor mana was now available but none came. Kolos untapped and played a [Primeval Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Primeval+Titan) which netted him another [Khalni Garden](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Khalni+Garden) and [Eldrazi Temple](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eldrazi+Temple). Once the Titan was in play, Froehlich sent it on a [Path to Exile](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Path+to+Exile) – Kolos finding a [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest).

The turn back to Froehlich, he sent in his Knight to get chumped by a Plant token before passing pack to Kolos. Kolos had an [Explore](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Explore) and a [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest), then played another [Primeval Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Primeval+Titan) – dumping [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge) and [Khalni Garden](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Khalni+Garden).

"I'm done if you're going to Path it." Kolos said of the Titan as he shuffled his library.

"No, that's fine." was Froehlich's cool response.

Froehlich sat thinking for a bit before playing [Ajani Vengeant](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ajani+Vengeant) and locking down Kolos's Chalice. On Kolos's turn he played a [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest) then [Ulamog, the Infinite Gyre](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ulamog%2C+the+Infinite+Gyre).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/0a/9f/0a9f79a1643f339e4ebe9af069dd0fb65305d25703b833bef8b58c5f7c19c78c.jpg)*Mythic Eldrazi: causing scooping since 2010.*

Froehlich just scooped in response.

Froehlich 1 – Kolos 2

**Game 4**

Froehlich liked his opener, and Kolos as well, so the land dropping began again. Froehlich: [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest). Kolos: [Khalni Garden](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Khalni+Garden). Froehlich: [Raging Ravine](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raging+Ravine). Kolos: [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest). Froehlich: [Mountain](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mountain).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/37/a7/37a7232986877d967e9ac76a1c836a4770e22acfa95a3e8be6c3377986cc4194.jpg)*Early action in ramp decks isn't often exciting but had far-reaching subtle implications for the game.*

On his third turn, however, Kolos had a [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest) for a [Cultivate](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cultivate) to grab two more. Froehlich matched it on his forth turn with a [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge) and [Garruk Wildspeaker](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Garruk+Wildspeaker), putting a 3/3 Beast token into play.

Kolos's [Mystifying Maze](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mystifying+Maze) with the mana up to use it served as his answer. Froehlich paused in the tank for a bit before using his [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge) on Kolos's Maze. The Beast token dived into the Plant token, and Froehlich upped Garruk's loyalty to play a [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary) before passing.

Kolos, too, took his time considering things and moved forward with an [Everflowing Chalice](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Everflowing+Chalice) for two, then used the colorless mana to active his own fresh [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge), knocking out Froehlich's Ravine.

Froehlich played a [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest) and used his Knight to transform it into [Arid Mesa](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arid+Mesa), then [Mountain](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mountain). Using all of the mana, including from the first [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest), [Destructive Force](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Destructive+Force) made it appearance, clearing away all but Garruk, the Knight, and Kolos's Chalice and lone [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest).

Kolos added a [Khalni Garden](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Khalni+Garden). Froehlich's Knight was chumped and a Beats joined the fray again. Kolos was mustering [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest)s, but the army at Froehlich's side continued to grow. As soon as Kolos hit six mana he cast [Primeval Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Primeval+Titan) which wasted no time finding two more [Khalni Garden](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Khalni+Garden)s.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/93/63/936394bd643867075ca21d607ed258e1706b666e6cc0413ad874e773d5d20720.jpg)*Khalni Gardens were buying Kolos much needed time.*

Froehlich had two Beasts and a massive 10/10 Knight, all of which obviously needed to attack, and Kolos worked the math hard. A Plant token jumped in front of the Knight while the [Primeval Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Primeval+Titan) went up to crush a Beast token, and the result of combat dropped Kolos to just 4 life. Froehlich finished his turn by replacing the lost Beast, adding a [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest) and playing an [Oblivion Ring](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Oblivion+Ring) which took away Kolos's Chalice.

Despite losing the Chalice, Kolos still had a healthy six mana and [Primeval Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Primeval+Titan) ready to swing. He sent his Titan in at Garruk, finding [Eldrazi Temple](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eldrazi+Temple) and [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge) and putting Garruk away. [Joraga Treespeaker](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Joraga+Treespeaker) joined the team for Kolos. Froehlich was undaunted as sent his two Beasts and Knight in – Kolos's Plant and Treespeaker serving as speed bumps. Froehlich then added a second Knight before passing.

Kolos tapped his abundant mana to cast [Terastodon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Terastodon), trading in his three [Khalni Garden](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Khalni+Garden)s for 3/3 Elephant tokens. Passing back, Froehlich only added a second [Raging Ravine](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Raging+Ravine) to his board.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/2e/5e/2e5eeb6ea62af0af46433f4a206749920823e1a0e604390306be66872079105e.jpg)*The tables were turning for Kolos favor.*

Kolos arranged his mana to keep [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge) ready, then sent his [Primeval Titan](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Primeval+Titan) into the red zone. [Eye of Ugin](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eye+of+Ugin) and [Tectonic Edge](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tectonic+Edge) jumped into play as Froehlich considered things. A [Knight of the Reliquary](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+the+Reliquary) rolled the Titan to the graveyard. After combat Kolos cast [Kozilek, Butcher of Truth](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kozilek%2C+Butcher+of+Truth) and drew four cards.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/05/5f/055fc4968e68aa323425d4bb9329c217f2dbda0aa5d4125843feafbe83f2bec3.jpg)*The Butcher of Truth didn't award an instant scoop for Kolos.*

Froehlich's eyes rolled back and forth across Kolos's board, his mind working the angles to handle the rapid changes that had occurred. He decided to play [Trace of Abundance](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Trace+of+Abundance) on his [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest) and passed with only one card left in hand.

Thanks to the Butcher, Kolos had many more in hand to work with. He started with [Explore](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Explore) and played [Eldrazi Temple](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Eldrazi+Temple) as his land for the turn – leaving his with the option of playing another – but hesitated before finally deciding to cast [Everflowing Chalice](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Everflowing+Chalice) with one counter and passing.

Froehlich tapped all his lands and played a Realm Razor. Kolos added mana to his pool and the board got a lot less cluttered. With the Razor in play Kolos cast a Summoner's Trap, finding an [Ulamog, the Infinite Gyre](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ulamog%2C+the+Infinite+Gyre). With the massive Eldrazi team up in place, Froehlich extended his hand.

**Froehlich 1 – Kolos 3**

## Quarterfinals – David Ochoa vs. Brad Nelson

*by Nate Price*

This quarterfinals match pitted two of the most recognizable members of the Channelfireball.com crew against one another. To my left sat David Ochoa, known for his "According to Webster" draft walkthrough series. His opponent was none other than the mountain man himself, professional Top 8 competitor Brad Nelson. Both players were sporting the spiffy new [Dredge](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dredge)-uh-Vine deck designed and championed by **Magic**thegathering.com's own Brian David-Marshall. The only difference between the two cards was a pair of cards in the sideboard, with Ochoa running [Pithing Needle](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pithing+Needle)s in place of Nelson's [Leyline of Vitality](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Leyline+of+Vitality).

Ochoa was forced to mulligan down to six, but found a reasonable hand to start things off. Both players started with similar draws. First came the [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch)s, followed soon after by a [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman). Ochoa added an extra [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise) to his side, key because his only lands were two [Forest](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Forest)s.

Nelson was the first to hit home, as a hasty [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) smashed over for four points of damage on turn three, accompanied by a [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman). Ochoa simply attacked back for one with his [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch) and expanded his mana with another pair of [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise). Nelson was the first to start using his [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman), discarding an [Extractor Demon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Extractor+Demon) to fetch out another [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine). This [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) soon joined the other and smashed over for eight. Sitting on thirteen life, Ochoa had to carefully think about how much damage he could take. Ultimately, he felt comfortable taking it all, dropping to five. At the end of his turn, Ochoa turned a [Merfolk Looter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Merfolk+Looter) into a [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine). While shuffling his deck afterwards, he was forced to comment, "How is it possible that we only have two cards in our graveyards combined." Nelson found the situation funny as well.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/1d/80/1d805b00f5e6414c21c5053842bc2f05e6beba0747b650b4ba914f3aa54b370b.jpg)*Brad Nelson frowns at his tiny, tiny graveyard.*

After untapping, Ochoa went back to work with his [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman), bringing the grand total to three cards in the graveyard, though the [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) he discarded didn't look fit to sit there for long. The [Merfolk Looter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Merfolk+Looter) he found himself immediately hit play alongside a [Sedraxis Alchemist](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sedraxis+Alchemist), returning the [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) and bouncing Nelson's [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman). This put Ochoa down to zero cards in hand. Nelson had four in his, but they were simply three lands and the Shaman that was just returned. He still had the [Extractor Demon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Extractor+Demon) in his yard, but Ochoa was careful to always keep a [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise) in the way.

Despite having his way blocked by the Birds, Nelson went for the Demon. Two copies of [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) and an [Extractor Demon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Extractor+Demon) pounded into Ochoa's board of Alchemist, [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine), Looter, and Birds. Two [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine)s traded and the [Extractor Demon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Extractor+Demon) ate a [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise). When the three creatures hit the bin, Nelson aimed all of his Demon's triggered abilities at himself, hitting a couple of Looters, a [Renegade Doppelganger](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Renegade+Doppelganger), and a [Hedron Crab](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hedron+Crab). Interestingly, Nelson didn't replay the [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman), despite having the mana.

Ochoa untapped, drew for his turn, and smiled. Grinning back at him from the top of the deck was an [Extractor Demon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Extractor+Demon), which Ochoa was more than happy to cast. On Nelson's turn, it became clear why he hadn't replayed the Shaman at the previous opportunity. After attacking with a 5/4 [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) and eating a [Sedraxis Alchemist](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sedraxis+Alchemist) (Ochoa milled nothing of consequence), Nelson tapped three mana to replay the Shaman alongside a new [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch). Thanks to the second creature, the [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) he had traded on the previous turn made a repeat appearance on his side. His board now stocked, Nelson passed the turn.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/7a/18/7a1830bb1f8008ccb233ce6260984f8a64206eaee31f50bda2afdf2f879aaa22.jpg)*That was a good draw!*

Ochoa thought for a minute after untapping. He peered at his only card in hand for a moment before attacking in with his Demon. Nelson just dropped to ten. Another moment of thought passed and Ochoa passed the turn. Now it was Nelson's turn to think.

"Declare attackers," he said, before sending his two [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine)s and [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman) across the table. Ochoa lined up his [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman) in front of Nelson's, and a mana critter in front of each of the [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine)s. When Nelson passed priority, Ochoa used his [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman) to fetch a second [Extractor Demon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Extractor+Demon) from his deck. With a [Merfolk Looter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Merfolk+Looter) and [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise) in play, he would be able to get it from his hand into his bin and unearth it for lethal damage. Nelson knew this and had no answer, conceding soon thereafter.

David Ochoa 1 – Brad Nelson 0

"I think I needed one more creature draw there," Nelson said with a pensive look on his face.

"Yeah, you were a little light there," Ochoa agreed.

"I only drew one spell that game, the Hierarch the turn after you bounced the [Fauna Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Fauna+Shaman)," Nelson said with a grimace.

Nelson started with a [Birds of Paradise](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Birds+of+Paradise) and a [Renegade Doppelganger](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Renegade+Doppelganger) over the first couple of turns. Ochoa matched this with a [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch) and [Merfolk Looter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Merfolk+Looter). Ochoa got the first point of damage in when he attacked on the second turn with his [Noble Hierarch](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Noble+Hierarch), but Nelson followed up with a much bigger swing. Renegade Doppelganger became a [Vengevine](https://web.archive.org/web/20210502212944/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Vengevine) and the pair rumbled over for eight.
